第一幕展现了那位坚定而英勇的共产党员，尽管面对父母的责难和妻子的唠叨，依然坚持自己的观点。这一幕还将那位无政府主义者弟弟（正如从布尔什维克对无政府主义的敌意中所能预料的那样）刻画为一个放荡不羁、游手好闲、一事无成的浪荡子，他狂热地爱着索尼娅（Sonia）这位年轻的资产阶级姑娘，如果得不到回报，这种爱很可能会变得危险。另一方面，她显然更青睐那位共产党员。很明显，后者也回应了她的爱，但并不十分明确他是否希望这种关系超越为共同理想服务的柏拉图式同志情谊。一场未能成功的罢工带来了匮乏和来自警方的危险，再加上无政府主义者日益强烈的嫉妒，引向了悲剧性的结局。我不太确定这一结局是如何具体促成的。所有暴力行动都是在舞台后进行的，这使得剧情有时难以捉摸。但似乎是无政府主义者在嫉妒的狂怒中伪造了一封来自他哥哥的信，约索尼娅幽会，并在那里杀害了她，同时向警方告发了他的哥哥。当警方前来实施逮捕，并指控哥哥也是最可能的凶手时，无政府主义者悔恨交加并供认了罪行。于是两人被一同带走。一旦勾勒出剧情梗概，这部剧本身便无需多作评论了。

===Original===

It had not great merit, though it is unwise to hazard a judgment on a play whose dialogue was not fully interpreted, but it was certainly real, and the link between audience and performers was established as it never seemed to be in the professional theatre. After the performance, the floor was cleared for dancing, and the audience were in a mood of thorough enjoyment.
The pageant of the “World Commune,” which was performed at the opening of the Third International Congress in Petrograd, was a still more important and significant phenomenon. I do not suppose that anything of the kind has been staged since the days of the medieval mystery plays. It was, in fact, a mystery play designed by the High Priests of the Communist faith to instruct the people. It was played on the steps of an immense white building that was once the Stock Exchange, a building with a classical colonnade on three sides of it, with a vast flight of steps in front, that did not extend the whole width of the building but left at each side a platform that was level with the floor of the colonnade. In front of this building a wide road ran from a bridge over one arm of the river to a bridge over the other, so that the stretches of water and sky on either side seemed to the eye of imagination like the painted wings of a gigantic stage. Two battered red columns of fantastic design, that were once light towers to guide ships, stood on either side midway between the extremities of the building and the water, but on the opposite side of the road. These two towers were beflagged and illuminated and carried the limelight, and between and behind them was gathered a densely packed audience of forty or fifty thousand people. The play began at sundown, while the sky was still red away to the right and the palaces on the far bank to the left still aglow with the setting sun, and it continued under the magic of the darkening sky. At first the beauty and grandeur of the setting drew the attention away from the performers, but gradually one became aware that on the platform before the columns kings and queens and courtiers in sumptuous conventional robes, and attended by soldiers, were conversing in dumb show with one another.

===Chinese===

这部剧并没有太高的艺术价值，尽管对一部对白未经充分翻译的戏剧轻易下结论是不明智的，但它无疑具有真实感，观众与演员之间建立起了在职业剧院中似乎从未见过的联系。演出结束后，场地被清理出来供大家跳舞，观众们沉浸在一片彻底欢畅的情绪之中。

在彼得格勒举行的第三国际（Third International）代表大会开幕式上上演的“世界公社”盛大庆典剧，则是一个更为重要且意义深远的现象。我认为自中世纪神秘剧时代以来，世上从未上演过类似规模的作品。事实上，这是一出由共产主义信仰的祭司们精心设计用于启蒙民众的神秘剧。它在曾作为证券交易所的一座宏伟白色建筑的台阶上演出，这座建筑三面环绕着古典式列柱廊，正前方有一片巨大的台阶，台阶并未延伸到建筑的整个宽度，而是在两侧各留出了一个与柱廊地面平齐的平台。在这座建筑的前方，一条宽阔的马路从河流的一条支流桥梁通往另一条支流桥梁，因此在想象力的眼中，两侧延伸的水面和天空宛如一座巨大舞台的彩绘侧幕。两座造型奇特的斑驳红色石柱——曾是用以引导船只的灯塔——耸立在马路对面，恰好位于建筑两端与水面之间的中间位置。这两座塔楼挂满了旗帜，灯火辉煌，并承载着舞台泛光灯，在塔楼之间和后方聚集着密集拥挤的四五万名观众。演出在日落时分拉开帷幕，当时右侧的天空依然泛着红光，而左侧对岸的宫殿在落日余晖中依然熠熠生辉，剧目在暮色渐浓的天空魔力下继续进行。起初，布景的优美与宏伟吸引了人们的注意力，使人忽视了表演者，但渐渐地人们发现在石柱前的平台上，身着华美传统长袍的国王、王后和朝臣们正在士兵的簇拥下互相比划着哑剧进行交谈。

===Original===

A few climbed the steps of a small wooden platform that was set up in the middle, and one indicated by a lifted hand that here should be built a monument to the power of capitalism over the earth. All gave signs of delight. Sentimental music was heard, and the gay company fell to waltzing away the hours. Meanwhile, from below on the road level, there streamed out of the darkness on either side of the building and up the half-lit steps, their fetters ringing in harmony with the music, the enslaved and toiling masses coming in response to command to build the monument for their masters. It is impossible to describe the exquisite beauty of the slow movement of those dark figures aslant the broad flight of steps; individual expressions were of course indistinguishable, and yet the movement and attitude of the groups conveyed pathos and patient endurance as well as any individual speech or gesture in the ordinary theatre. Some groups carried hammer and anvil, and others staggered under enormous blocks of stone. Love for the ballet has perhaps made the Russians understand the art of moving groups of actors in unison. As I watched these processions climbing the steps in apparently careless and spontaneous fashion, and yet producing so graceful a result, I remembered the mad leap of the archers down the stage in *Prince Igor*, which is also apparently careless and spontaneous and full of wild and irregular beauty, yet never varies a hair-breadth from one performance to the next.
For a time the workers toiled in the shadow in their earthly world, and dancing continued in the lighted paradise of the rulers above, until presently, in sign that the monument was complete, a large yellow disc was hoisted amid acclamation above the highest platform between the columns. But at the same moment a banner was uplifted amongst the people, and a small figure was seen gesticulating. Angry fists were shaken and the banner and speaker disappeared, only to reappear almost immediately in another part of the dense crowd. Again hostility, until finally among the French workers away up on the right, the first Communist manifesto found favour. Rallying around their banner the communards ran shouting down the steps, gathering supporters as they came.

===Chinese===

有几个人登上了设在中央的小木台的台阶，一人举起手示意应当在此建造一座纪念资本主义统治大地的纪念碑。所有人都露出了欢悦的神情。伤感的音乐响起，这群衣着华丽的人便开始跳起华尔兹消磨时光。与此同时，从下方马路平面的建筑两侧黑暗中，被奴役的劳苦大众涌上泛着微光的台阶，他们身上的镣铐随着音乐叮当作响，遵照命令前来为他们的主子建造纪念碑。那些黑色身影斜倚在宽阔台阶上缓缓移动的绝美姿态，是无法用言语形容的；个人的表情自然无法辨认，然而群体的动作与姿态所传达出的悲壮与坚忍耐力，绝不逊色于普通剧院中任何个人的言语或手势。一些群体携带着铁锤与铁砧，另一些群体则在巨大的石块下步履蹒跚。对芭蕾舞的热爱或许使俄国人理解了让演员群体步调一致行动的艺术。当我看着这些队列以看似漫不经心、自发随性的方式攀登台阶，却产生出如此优雅的效果时，我想起了《伊戈尔王》（*Prince Igor*）中弓箭手跳下舞台时的疯狂一跃——那同样看似随性自发且充满狂野不羁的美感，但在一次又一次的演出中却从未产生过一丝一毫的偏差。

在一段时间里，工人们在阴影中属于他们的人世间辛勤劳作，而在上方统治者灯火辉煌的天堂里舞蹈仍在继续，直到不久后，作为纪念碑完工的标志，一个巨大的黄色圆盘在欢呼声中被升到柱廊之间的最高平台上。但就在同一时刻，一面旗帜在人群中升起，可以看到一个小巧的身影在手舞足蹈地演说。愤怒的拳头在空中挥舞，旗帜和演讲者旋即消失，却几乎立刻又在密密麻麻的人群的另一处重新出现。敌对情绪再次蔓延，直到最后在右上方远处的法国工人中间，第一份《共产党宣言》获得了赞同。公社社员们聚集在他们的旗帜周围，一边高呼一边奔下台阶，沿途不断汇聚支持者。

===Original===

Above, all is confusion, kings and queens scuttling in unroyal fashion with flying velvet robes to safe citadels right and left, while the army prepares to defend the main citadel of capitalism with its golden disc of power. The communards scale the steps to the fortress which they finally capture, haul down the disc and set their banner in its place. The merry music of the “Carmagnole” is heard, and the victors are seen expressing their delight by dancing first on one foot and then on the other, like marionettes. Below, the masses dance with them in a frenzy of joy. But a pompous procession of Prussian legions is seen approaching, and, amid shrieks and wails of despair, the people are driven back, and their leaders set in a row and shot. Thereafter came one of the most moving scenes in the drama. Several dark-clad women appeared carrying a black pall supported on sticks, which they set in front of the bodies of the leaders so that it stood out, an irregular pointed black shape against the white columns behind. But for this melancholy monument the stage was now empty. Thick clouds of black smoke arose from braziers on either side and obscured the steps and the platform. Through the smoke came the distant sound of Chopin’s *Marche Funèbre*, and as the air became clearer white figures could be dimly seen moving around the black pall in a solemn dance of mourning. Behind them the columns shone ghostly and unreal against the glimmering mauve rays of an uncertain and watery dawn.
The second part of the pageant opened in July 1914. Once again the rulers were feasting and the workers at toil, but the scene was enlivened by the presence of the leaders of the Second International, a group of decrepit professorial old men, who waddled in in solemn procession carrying tomes full of international learning. They sat in a row between the rulers and the people, deep in study, spectacles on nose. The call to war was the signal for a dramatic appeal from the workers to these leaders, who refused to accept the Red Flag, but weakly received patriotic flags from their respective governments. Jaurès, elevated to be the symbol of protest, towered above the people, crying in a loud voice, but fell back immediately as the assassin’s shot rang out. Then the people divided into their national groups and the war began.

===Chinese===

上方顿时陷入一片混乱，国王和王后们毫无君王风度地拖着飘动的丝绒长袍向左右两侧安全的城堡逃窜，而军队则准备保卫带有象征权力的金色圆盘的资本主义主城堡。公社社员们攀上通往要塞的台阶并最终攻克了它，扯下圆盘，将他们的旗帜立在原处。《卡马尼奥拉》（*Carmagnole*）的欢快音乐响起，可以看到胜利者们像木偶一样先用一只脚跳、接着又用另一只脚跳，以此表达内心的狂喜。下方的大众也随着他们陷入一片狂欢的热舞之中。然而，一支傲慢威严的普鲁士军团队列正逐渐逼近，在一阵阵绝望的尖叫与哀号声中，民众被击退，他们的领袖被排成一排当场枪决。随后出现了全剧中最令人动容的一幕之一。几位身着黑衣的妇女走上台前，用木棍撑起一面黑色棺罩，她们将棺罩立在领袖们的遗体前，使其作为一个不规则的尖顶黑色轮廓，在后方白色石柱的衬托下格外显眼。除了这座忧伤的纪念碑外，舞台此刻空无一人。两侧火盆升腾起浓密的黑烟，遮蔽了台阶和平台。穿透黑烟，远处传来了肖邦《葬礼进行曲》（*Marche Funèbre*）的声音；随着烟雾逐渐消散，隐约可见白色的身影围绕着黑色棺罩起舞，跳着庄严肃穆的哀悼之舞。在他们身后，石柱在黎明那充满疑虑与水汽的微弱淡紫色晨光映衬下，显得幽幻而虚真莫辨。

戏剧的第二部分拉开帷幕于 1914 年 7 月。统治者再次享乐，工人们再次劳作，但第二国际（Second International）领导人的出现使场面活跃起来，这是一群衰老颓唐、具有教授派头的老头，他们迈着蹒跚的步子庄严列队走来，手里抱着装满国际学问的大部头著作。他们戴着眼镜，埋头研读，在统治者与人民之间坐成一排。战争的呼号成了工人们向这些领袖发出极具戏剧性呼吁的信号，而这些领袖拒绝接受红旗，却软弱地从各自政府手中接过了爱国主义旗帜。作为抗议象征被推举出来的让·饶勒斯（Jaurès）高高耸立在人民之上，大声疾呼，但在刺客的枪声响起后立刻倒下。随后人民分裂成各自的民族阵营，战争爆发了。

===Original===

It was at this point that “God Save the King” was played as the English soldiers marched out, in a comic manner which made one think of it as “*Gawd* save the King.” Other national anthems were burlesqued in a similar fashion, but none quite so successfully. A ridiculous effigy of the Tsar with a knout in his hand now occupied the symbolic position and dominated the scene. The incidents of the war which affected Russia were then played. Spectacular cavalry charges on the road, marching soldiers, batteries of artillery, a pathetic procession of cripples and nurses, and other scenes too numerous to describe, made up that part of the pageant devoted to the war.
Then came the Russian Revolution in all its stages. Cars dashed by full of armed men, red flags appeared everywhere, the people stormed the citadel and hauled down the effigy of the Tsar. The Kerensky Government assumed control and drove them forth to war again, but soon they returned to the charge, destroyed the Provisional Government, and hoisted all the emblems of the Russian Soviet Republic. The Entente leaders, however, were seen preparing their troops for battle, and the pageant went on to show the formation of the Red Army under its emblem the Red Star. White figures with golden trumpets appeared foretelling victory for the proletariat. The last scene, the World Commune, is described in the words of the abstract, taken from a Russian newspaper, as follows:﻿—

> Cannon shots announce the breaking of the blockade against Soviet Russia, and the victory of the World Proletariat. The Red Army returns from the front, and passes in triumphant review before the leaders of the Revolution. At their feet lie the crowns of kings and the gold of the bankers. Ships draped with flags are seen carrying workers from the west. The workers of the whole world, with the emblems of labour, gather for the celebration of the World Commune. In the heavens luminous inscriptions in different languages appear, greeting the Congress: “Long live the Third International! Workers of the world, unite! Triumph to the sounds of the hymn of the World Commune, the ‘International.’ ”

===Chinese===

正是在此时，当英国士兵列队开拔时，现场奏响了《天佑吾王》（God Save the King），其滑稽滑稽的方式让人联想到“天‘佑’吾王”。其他国家的国歌也以类似的方式遭到戏仿，但没有一首能达到如此成功的效果。一个手持皮鞭的沙皇荒唐丑怪的人形雕像此时占据了象征性的中心位置并统摄着全场。随后上演了波及俄国的战争事件。马路上壮观的骑兵冲锋、行进的士兵、成排的炮兵连、残废者与护士凄惨悲凉的队列，以及多得难以一一赘述的其他场景，构成了这出庆典剧中表现战争的部分。

紧接着是各个阶段的俄国革命（Russian Revolution）。满载武装人员的汽车疾驰而过，红旗遍地招展，民众攻占城堡并将沙皇雕像扯下。克伦斯基政府（Kerensky Government）接管了政权，并驱使他们再次走向战争，但不久他们卷土重来，摧毁了临时政府（Provisional Government），升起了俄罗斯苏维埃共和国（Russian Soviet Republic）的所有象征徽记。然而，协约国领袖被展现为正在整军备战，剧目接着展现了在红星徽章指引下红军（Red Army）的组建。手持金色号角的白衣身影出现，预示着无产阶级的胜利。最后一幕“世界公社”，用俄国报纸上摘录的提要文字描述如下：

> 礼炮轰鸣，宣告对苏维埃俄国的封锁已被打破，世界无产阶级取得了胜利。红军从前线凯旋，在革命领袖面前举行胜利检阅。在他们的脚下躺着君王的王冠与银行家的黄金。挂满旗帜的船只载着来自西方的工人驶来。全世界的劳动者携带着劳动的徽记，聚集在一起庆祝世界公社的诞生。天空中出现了不同语言的明亮题词，向大会致意：“第三国际万岁！全世界无产者，联合起来！在世界公社赞歌《国际歌》的旋律中走向胜利！”

===Original===

Even so glowing an account, however, hardly does it justice. It had the pomp and majesty of the Day of Judgment itself. Rockets climbed the skies and peppered them with a thousand stars, fireworks blazed on all sides, garlanded and beflagged ships moved up and down the river, chariots bearing the emblems of prosperity, grapes and corn, travelled slowly along the road. The Eastern peoples came carrying gifts and emblems. The actors, massed upon the steps, waved triumphant hands, trumpets sounded, and the song of the “International” from ten thousand throats rose like a mighty wave engulfing the whole.
Though the end of this drama may have erred on the side of the grandiose, this may perhaps be forgiven the organizers in view of the occasion for which they prepared it. Nothing, however, could detract from the beauty and dramatic power of the opening and of many of the scenes. Moreover, the effects obtained by movement in the mass were almost intoxicating. The first entrance of the masses gave a sense of dumb and patient force that was moving in the extreme, and the frenzied delight of the dancing crowd at the victory of the French communards stirred one to ecstasy. The pageant lasted for five hours or more, and was as exhausting emotionally as the Passion Play is said to be. I had the vision of a great period of Communist art, more especially of such open-air spectacles, which should have the grandeur and scope and eternal meaning of the plays of ancient Greece, the medieval mysteries, or the Shakespearean theatre. In building, writing, acting, even in painting, work would be done, as it once was, by groups, not by one hand or mind, and evolution would proceed slowly until once again the individual emerged from the mass.
In considering Education under the Bolshevik regime, the same two factors which I have already dealt with in discussing art, namely industrial development and the communist doctrine, must be taken into account. Industrial development is in reality one of the tenets of Communism, but as it is one which in Russia is likely to endanger the doctrine as a whole I have thought it better to consider it as a separate item.
As in the matter of art, so in education, those who have given unqualified praise seem to have taken the short and superficial view.

===Chinese===

然而，即便如此热烈的记述，也几乎难以完全展现其恢弘气象。它具备了如同最后的审判本身一般的宏伟与庄严。火箭直射苍穹，散落成千万颗星辰，焰火在四处绽放，缠绕着花环、挂满旗帜的船只在河面上往来穿梭，满载着繁荣徽记——葡萄与玉米——的彩车在马路上缓缓行驶。东方各族人民带着礼物和象征物纷至沓来。聚集在台阶上的演员们挥舞着胜利的双手，号角齐鸣，万人喉咙中迸发出的《国际歌》声浪犹如排山倒海的巨浪吞没了一切。

尽管这出戏剧的结尾可能偏向于过度宏大，但鉴于组织者筹备此剧的重大场合，这或许是可以原谅的。然而，没有任何东西能够减损序幕以及诸多场景的美感与戏剧力量。此外，由群体动作所呈现的效果几乎令人如痴如醉。大众的首次登场展现出一种极具感染力、令人动容的沉默而坚忍的力量，而欢庆法国公社社员胜利的狂热人群舞蹈更激起了人们的狂喜。这场盛大庆典剧持续了五个小时以上，正如人们所说的那样，在情感上的消耗不亚于耶稣受难剧。我仿佛看到了一个伟大的共产主义艺术时期的愿景，尤其是这种户外盛大景观，它应当具备古希腊戏剧、中世纪神秘剧或莎士比亚戏剧的宏伟气魄、辽阔视野与永恒意蕴。在建筑、写作、演艺乃至绘画中，作品将像古时一样由群体完成，而非出自单一个人的手笔或心智，演进将缓慢进行，直到个体再次从大众中脱颖而出。

在审视布尔什维克政权下的教育时，必须考虑到我在探讨艺术时已经涉及的同样两个因素，即工业发展与共产主义学说。工业发展实际上是共产主义的信条之一，但由于在俄国这一信条很可能会危及整个学说本身，我认为将其作为单独一项来考察更为妥当。

正如在艺术问题上一样，在教育方面，那些给予毫无保留赞扬的人似乎也采取了短视和肤浅的视角。

===Original===

It is hardly necessary to launch into descriptions of the crèches, country homes or palaces for children, where Montessori methods prevail, where the pupils cultivate their little gardens, model in plasticine, draw and sing and act, and dance their Eurythmic dances barefoot on floors once sacred to the tread of the nobility. I saw a reception and distributing house in Petrograd with which no fault could be found from the point of view of scientific organization. The children were bright-eyed and merry, and the rooms airy and clean. I saw, too, a performance by school children in Moscow which included some quite wonderful Eurythmic dancing, in particular an interpretation of Grieg’s “Tanz in der Halle des Bergkönigs” by the Dalcroze method, but with a colour and warmth which were Russian, and in odd contrast to the mathematical precision associated with most Dalcroze performances.
But in spite of the obvious merit of such institutions as exist, misgivings would arise. To begin with, it must be remembered that it is necessary first to admit that children should be delivered up almost entirely to the State. Nominally, the mother still comes to see her child in these schools, but in actual fact, the drafting of children to the country must intervene, and the whole temper of the authorities seemed to be directed towards breaking the link between mother and child. To some this will seem an advantage, and it is a point which admits of lengthy discussion, but as it belongs rather to the question of women and the family under Communism, I can do no more than mention it here.
Then, again, it must be remembered that the tactics of the Bolsheviks towards such schools as existed under the old regime in provincial towns and villages, have not been the same as their tactics towards the theatres. The greater number of these schools are closed, in part, it would seem, from lack of personnel, and in part from fear of counterrevolutionary propaganda. The result is that, though those schools which they have created are good and organized on modern lines, on the whole there would seem to be less diffusion of child education than before. In this, as in most other departments, the Bolsheviks show themselves loath to attempt anything which cannot be done on a large scale and impregnated with Communist doctrine.

===Chinese===

几乎没有必要去详尽描述托儿所、乡村疗养所或儿童宫殿，在这些地方盛行着蒙台梭利（Montessori）教学法，学生们在其中耕作他们的小花园，用橡皮泥捏制模型，绘画、唱歌和演戏，并在曾经专供贵族践踏的地板上赤足跳着韵律操舞。我在彼得格勒（Petrograd）看到了一处儿童收容与分流所，从科学组织的角度来看，它是无可挑剔的。孩子们眼神明亮、欢快活泼，房间空气流通、干净整洁。我在莫斯科（Moscow）也看到了一场由学童演出的节目，其中包括一些相当精彩的韵律操舞，特别是用达尔克罗兹（Dalcroze）方法对格里格《在山魔王的宫殿中》（Tanz in der Halle des Bergkönigs）的演绎，但带有一种属于俄国的色彩与热情，与大多数达尔克罗兹式表演所特有的数学般的精确形成了奇特的对比。

然而，尽管这些现存机构具有明显的优点，疑虑却不由自主地产生。首先，必须记住，人们首先必须承认儿童应当几乎完全交给国家抚养。名义上，母亲仍然可以到这些学校探望她的孩子，但实际上，儿童被调往乡村必然会介入其中，而且当局的整个意图似乎都致力于切断母子之间的纽带。对某些人来说，这似乎是一个优点，这是一个可以进行漫长讨论的问题，但由于它更属于共产主义下妇女与家庭的问题，我在此只能稍作提及。

再者，必须记住，布尔什维克对待外省城镇和乡村旧政权下遗留的这类学校的策略，与他们对待剧院的策略并不相同。这些学校的大多数都已关闭，部分似乎是由于缺乏师资，部分是出于对反革命宣传的恐惧。结果是，尽管他们创办的那些学校质量很好且按照现代方式组织，但总体而言，儿童教育的普及程度似乎比以往任何时候都要低。在这一点上，正如在大多数其他部门一样，布尔什维克表现出不愿尝试任何无法进行大规模推广、且无法完全灌输共产主义教义的事物。

===Original===

It goes without saying that Communist doctrine is taught in schools, as Christianity has been taught hitherto, moreover the Communist teachers show bitter hostility to other teachers who do not accept the doctrine. At the children’s entertainment alluded to above, the dances and poems performed had nearly all some close relation to Communism, and a teacher addressed the children for something like an hour and a half on the duties of Communists and the errors of Anarchism.
This teaching of Communism, however necessary it may appear for the building of the Communist state of the future, does seem to me to be an evil in that it is done emotionally and fanatically, with an appeal to hate and militant ardour rather than to constructive reason. It binds the free intellect and destroys initiative. An industrial state needs not only obedient and patient workers and artists, it needs also men and women with initiative in scientific research. It is idle to provide channels for scientific research later if it is to be choked at the source. That source is an enquiring and free intellect unhampered by iron dogma. Beneficial to artistic and emotional development therefore, the teaching of Communism as a faith may well be most pernicious to the scientific and intellectual side of education, and will lead direct to the pragmatist view of knowledge and scientific research which the Church and the capitalist already find it so convenient to adopt.
But to come to the chief and most practical question, the relation of education to industry. Sooner or later education in Russia must become subordinate to the needs of industrial development. That the Bolsheviks already realize this is proved by the articles of Lunacharsky which recently appeared in *Le Phare* (Geneva). It was the spectre of industry that haunted me throughout the consideration of education as in the consideration of art, and what I have said above of its dangers to the latter seems to me also to apply here. Montessori schools belong, in my view, to that stage in industrial development when education is directed as much towards leisure occupations as towards preparation for professional life. Possibly the fine flower of useless scientific enquiry belongs to this stage also.

===Chinese===

不言而喻，共产主义教义在学校里被传授，正如基督教迄今为止被传授一样，而且共产党员教师对其他不接受该教义的教师表现出强烈的敌意。在上述提到的儿童文娱活动中，表演的舞蹈和诗歌几乎都与共产主义有着密切的联系，一位教师向孩子们讲解了大约一个半小时关于共产党员的职责和无政府主义的错误。

这种对共产主义的灌输，尽管对于建设未来的共产主义国家而言似乎是必不可少的，但在我看来却确实是一种弊病，因为它是以充满情感和狂热的方式进行的，诉诸仇恨与好战的热情，而非建设性的理性。它束缚了自由的智力，扼杀了首创精神。一个工业国家不仅需要顺从且耐劳的工人和艺术家，还需要在科学研究中具有首创精神的男女。如果在源头就将科学研究扼杀，日后再提供渠道也是徒劳的。那个源头就是不受铁的教条束缚的探究与自由的心智。因此，尽管将共产主义作为一种信仰来灌输对艺术和情感的发展有益，但对教育中科学与智力的一面却可能极其有害，并将直接导致实用主义的知识观与科研观——教会与资本家早就发现采用这种观点是何等便利。

然而，谈到最主要也是最现实的问题，即教育与工业的关系。俄国的教育迟早必须服从于工业发展的需求。布尔什维克已经认识到了这一点，卢那察尔斯基（Lunacharsky）最近发表在日内瓦《灯塔》（*Le Phare*）杂志上的文章就证明了这一点。在整个考察教育的过程中，正如在考察艺术的过程中一样，工业的幽灵始终萦绕在我的心头，而我上面所谈到的工业对艺术的危险，在我看来也同样适用于这里。在我看来，蒙台梭利学校属于工业发展的那样一个阶段，即教育不仅面向职业生活的准备，同样也面向闲暇消遣。无实用价值的纯科学探究的绚丽之花，或许也属于这一阶段。

===Original===

Nobody in Russia is likely to have much leisure for a good many years to come, if the Bolshevik programme of industrial development is efficiently carried out. And there seemed to me to be something pathetic and almost cruel in this varied and agreeable education of the child, when one reflected on the long hours of grinding toil to which he was soon to be subject in workshop or factory. For I repeat that I do not believe industrial work in the early days of industry can be made tolerable to the worker. Once again I experienced the dread of seeing the ideals of the Russian revolutionaries go down before the logic of necessity. They are beginning to pride themselves on being hard, practical men, and it seems quite reasonable to fear that they should come to regard this full and humane development of the child as a mere luxury and ultimately neglect it. Worse still, the few of these schools which already exist may perhaps become exclusive to the Communists and their children, or that company of Samurai which is to leaven and govern the mass of the people. If so, they will soon come to resemble our public schools, in that they will prepare, in an artificial play atmosphere, men who will pass straight to the position of leaders, while the portion of the proletariat who serve under them will be reading and writing, just so much technical training as is necessary, and Communist doctrine.
This is a nightmare hypothesis, but the difficulties of the practical problem seem to warrant its entertainment. The number of people in Russia who can even read and write is extremely small, the need to get them employed industrially as rapidly as possible is very great, hence the system of education which develops out of this situation cannot be very ambitious or enlightened. Further it will have to continue over a sufficiently long period of time to allow of the risk of its becoming stable and traditional. In adult education already the pupil comes for a short period, learns Communism, reading and writing﻿—there is hardly time to give him much more﻿—and returns to leaven the army or his native village. In achieving this the Bolsheviks are already doing a very important and valuable work, but they cannot hope for a long while to become the model of public instruction which they have hitherto been represented to be.

===Chinese===

如果布尔什维克的工业发展计划得到有效执行，在未来很多年里，俄国几乎没有人可能拥有多少闲暇。当人们想到这个孩子不久后将在工场或工厂中遭受长时间繁重的苦役时，这种丰富多样且愉悦的儿童教育在我看来就带有某种凄凉甚至残忍的意味。因为我重申，我不相信在工业发展的初期阶段，工业劳动能让工人感到可以忍受。我再一次体验到了看到俄国革命者的理想在必然性的逻辑面前屈服的恐惧。他们开始以身为坚强、务实的人而自豪，而相当合理的担忧是，他们最终会把儿童这种全面而人道的成长视为一种纯粹的奢侈品并最终将其忽视。更为严重的是，目前已经存在的少数这类学校，可能会变成专门面向共产党员及其子女、或者面向那群旨在启迪和统治广大人民群众的“武士阶层”（Samurai）的特权机构。如果是这样，它们很快就会变得类似于我们的公立学校，因为它们将在一种人为的游戏氛围中培养出将直接跃升为领袖地位的人物，而在他们手下服务的无产阶级群体，则将仅仅学习读写、掌握维持生计所必需的最低限度技术培训以及共产主义教义。

这是一个噩梦般的假设，但实际问题的艰难险阻似乎证明了这种推测的合理性。在俄国，甚至能够识字读写的人数都极其稀少，尽快让他们投入工业就业的需求又极其迫切，因此在这一局面下发展起来的教育体制不可能具有宏大的雄心或开明的视野。此外，它必须持续相当长的一段时期，以至于面临着演变为稳固传统体制的风险。在成人教育中，学员来校学习很短的一段时间，学习共产主义与识字读写——几乎没有时间传授给他更多的东西——随后便返回部队或家乡去启发军队或他的故乡。在实现这一点上，布尔什维克已经在从事一项非常重要且极具价值的工作，但他们不可能在短时间内指望成为人们迄今为止所描绘的那种国民教育的典范。

===Original===

And the conditions of their becoming so ultimately are adherence to their ideals through a very long period of stress, and a lessening of fanaticism in their Communist teaching, conditions which, unhappily, seem to be mutually incompatible.
The whole of the argument set out in this chapter may be summed up in the statement of one fact which the mere idealist is prone to overlook, namely that Russia is a country at a stage in economic development not much more advanced than America in the pioneer days. The old civilization was aristocratic and exotic; it could not survive in the modern world. It is true that it produced great men, but its foundations were rotten. The new civilization may, for the moment, be less productive of individual works of genius, but it has a new solidity and gives promise of a new unity. It may be that I have taken too hopeful a view and that the future evolution of Russia will have as little connection with the life and tradition of its present population as modern America with the life of the Red Indian tribes. The fact that there exists in Russia a population at a far higher stage of culture, which will be industrially educated, not exterminated, militates against this hypothesis, but the need for education may make progress slower than it was in the United States.
One would not have looked for the millennium of Communism, nor even for valuable art and educational experiment in the America of early railroading and farming days. Nor must one look for such things from Russia yet. It may be that during the next hundred years there, economic evolution will obscure Communist ideals, until finally, in a country that has reached the stage of present-day America, the battle will be fought out again to a victorious and stable issue. Unless, indeed, the Marxian scripture prove to be not infallible, and faith and heroic devotion show themselves capable of triumphing over economic necessity.

===Chinese===

而他们最终实现这一目标的条件，是在极其漫长的困厄时期中坚守其理想，并在共产主义教学中减少狂热成分，然而令人遗憾的是，这些条件似乎是相互矛盾的。

本章提出的全部论点，可以概括为一个纯粹的理想主义者容易忽视的事实陈述，即俄国是一个在经济发展阶段上并不比拓荒时期的美国先进多少的国家。旧文明是贵族式的、舶来的；它无法在现代世界中生存下去。诚然，它造就了伟大的人物，但它的基石早已腐朽。新文明就目前而言或许较少产生个体的天才作品，但它具有一种新的坚固性，并有望带来新的团结。也许我的看法过于乐观，俄国未来的演进可能与它当前人口的生活和传统几乎毫无关联，正如现代美国与印第安红人部落的生活毫无关联一样。在俄国存在着处于高得多的文化阶段的人口，他们将接受工业教育而非遭到灭绝，这一事实反驳了这种假设，但对教育的需求可能会使那里的进步比在美国更为缓慢。

在早期修建铁路和农耕开垦时代的美国，人们不会指望出现共产主义的千禧盛世，甚至不会指望出现有价值的艺术与教育实验。人们同样不能指望从当下的俄国获得这些东西。也许在未来的这一百年里，那里的经济演进将掩盖共产主义的理想，直到最后，在一个达到了当今美国发展阶段的国家里，这场战役将再次打响并取得胜利与稳定的结果。除非，马克思主义的经典确实并非颠扑不破，而信仰与英勇的奉献精神被证明能够战胜经济必然性。

## V Communism and the Soviet Constitution / V 共产主义与苏维埃宪法
===Original===

Before I went to Russia I imagined that I was going to see an interesting experiment in a new form of representative government. I did see an interesting experiment, but not in representative government. Everyone who is interested in Bolshevism knows the series of elections, from the village meeting to the All-Russian Soviet, by which the people’s commissaries are supposed to derive their power. We were told that, by the recall, the occupational constituencies, and so on, a new and far more perfect machinery had been devised for ascertaining and registering the popular will. One of the things we hoped to study was the question whether the Soviet system is really superior to Parliamentarism in this respect.
We were not able to make any such study, because the Soviet system is moribund.4 No conceivable system of free election would give majorities to the Communists, either in town or country. Various methods are therefore adopted for giving the victory to Government candidates. In the first place, the voting is by show of hands, so that all who vote against the Government are marked men. In the second place, no candidate who is not a Communist can have any printing done, the printing works being all in the hands of the State. In the third place, he cannot address any meetings, because the halls all belong to the State. The whole of the press is, of course, official; no independent daily is permitted. In spite of all these obstacles, the Mensheviks have succeeded in winning about 40 seats out of 1,500 on the Moscow Soviet, by being known in certain large factories where the electoral campaign could be conducted by word of mouth. They won, in fact, every seat that they contested.
But although the Moscow Soviet is nominally sovereign in Moscow, it is really only a body of electors who choose the executive committee of forty, out of which, in turn, is chosen the Presidium, consisting of nine men who have all the power. The Moscow Soviet, as a whole, meets rarely; the Executive Committee is supposed to meet once a week, but did not meet while we were in Moscow. The Presidium, on the contrary, meets daily. Of course, it is easy for the Government to exercise pressure over the election of the executive committee, and again over the election of the Presidium.

===Chinese===

在我前往俄国之前，我曾设想自己将亲眼目睹一场关于代议制政府新形式的有趣实验。我确实目睹了一场有趣的实验，但并非发生在代议制政府领域。每一个对布尔什维主义（Bolshevism）感兴趣的人，都知晓从村级会议到全俄苏维埃（All-Russian Soviet）的一系列选举程序，人民委员们据称正是从这一程序中获得权力的。曾有人告诉我们，通过罢免权、职业选区等等机制，人们已经设计出一种用于查明和记录民众意愿的崭新且更加完善的机制。我们希望研究的课题之一，就是苏维埃制度在这方面是否确实优越于议会主义（Parliamentarism）。

我们未能进行任何此类研究，因为苏维埃制度已经濒临死亡。4 无论在城市还是乡村，任何可以设想的自由选举制度都绝不可能让共产党人获得多数席位。因此，当局采取了各种方法来让政府候选人获胜。首先，投票是通过举手表决进行的，因此所有投票反对政府的人都会被标记出来。其次，任何非共产党员的候选人都不可能印发任何材料，因为印刷厂全部掌握在国家手中。第三，他无法举行任何集会，因为所有礼堂都归国家所有。当然，所有的出版报刊都是官方性质的；没有任何独立的日报被获准发行。尽管存在所有这些障碍，孟什维克依然通过在某些可以通过口耳相传进行竞选宣传的大型工厂中享有知名度，在莫斯科苏维埃（Moscow Soviet）的 1,500 个席位中成功赢得了大约 40 个席位。事实上，凡是他们参与竞选的席位，他们全部获胜了。

然而，尽管莫斯科苏维埃名义上在莫斯科拥有最高权力，但它实际上只是一个选举团，负责选出由四十人组成的执行委员会（Executive Committee），再从执行委员会中选出由掌握全部权力的九人组成的主席团（Presidium）。作为一个整体的莫斯科苏维埃极少召开会议；执行委员会理应每周开会一次，但在我们逗留莫斯科期间从未开过会。相反，主席团每天都举行会议。当然，政府在执行委员会的选举中施加压力是轻而易举的，在主席团的选举中同样如此。

===Original===

It must be remembered that effective protest is impossible, owing to the absolutely complete suppression of free speech and free Press. The result is that the Presidium of the Moscow Soviet consists only of orthodox Communists.
Kamenev, the President of the Moscow Soviet, informed us that the recall is very frequently employed; he said that in Moscow there are, on an average, thirty recalls a month. I asked him what were the principal reasons for the recall, and he mentioned four: drinking, going to the front (and being, therefore, incapable of performing the duties), change of politics on the part of the electors, and failure to make a report to the electors once a fortnight, which all members of the Soviet are expected to do. It is evident that the recall affords opportunities for governmental pressure, but I had no chance of finding out whether it is used for this purpose.
In country districts the method employed is somewhat different. It is impossible to secure that the village Soviet shall consist of Communists, because, as a rule, at any rate in the villages I saw, there are no Communists. But when I asked in the villages how they were represented on the Volost (the next larger area) or the Guberniya, I was met always with the reply that they were not represented at all. I could not verify this, and it is probably an overstatement, but all concurred in the assertion that if they elected a non-Communist representative he could not obtain a pass on the railway and, therefore, could not attend the Volost or Guberniya Soviet. I saw a meeting of the Guberniya Soviet of Saratov. The representation is so arranged that the town workers have an enormous preponderance over the surrounding peasants; but even allowing for this, the proportion of peasants seemed astonishingly small for the centre of a very important agricultural area.
The All-Russian Soviet, which is constitutionally the supreme body, to which the People’s Commissaries are responsible, meets seldom, and has become increasingly formal. Its sole function at present, so far as I could discover, is to ratify, without discussion, previous decisions of the Communist Party on matters (especially concerning foreign policy) upon which the constitution requires its decision.
All real power is in the hands of the Communist Party, who number about 600,000 in a population of about 120 millions.

===Chinese===

必须记住的是，由于言论自由和新闻出版自由遭到了彻头彻尾的完全取缔，进行有效的抗议是根本不可能的。结果就是，莫斯科苏维埃的主席团完全由正统的共产党员组成。

莫斯科苏维埃主席加米涅夫（Kamenev）告知我们，罢免权被非常频繁地使用；他说在莫斯科平均每月有三十次罢免。我问他罢免的主要原因是什么，他提到了四点：酗酒、开赴前线（从而无法履行职责）、选民政治立场的改变，以及未能做到每两周向选民汇报一次工作——而这是所有苏维埃成员都必须履行的职责。显而易见，罢免权为政府施加压力提供了机会，但我没有机会查明它是否确实被用于这一目的。

在农村地区，所采用的方法略有不同。确保村苏维埃由共产党员组成是不可能的，因为通常来说——无论如何在我见过的村庄里——根本就没有共产党员。但当我在村庄里询问他们在乡（沃尔斯特，Volost，即上一级行政区划）或省（Guberniya）苏维埃中是如何被代表的时，我得到的回答始终是他们根本没有任何代表。我无法核实这一点，这或许是夸大其词，但所有人都一致断言：如果他们选举了一名非共产党员代表，该代表就无法获得火车通行证，从而无法出席乡或省苏维埃会议。我旁听过一次萨拉托夫（Saratov）省苏维埃的会议。那里的代表名额分配被安排成使城市工人比周边农民占据压倒性优势；但即便考虑到这一点，对于一个极其重要的农业区中心而言，农民代表的比例仍然低得令人吃惊。

全俄苏维埃在宪法上是人民委员向其负责的最高权力机构，但它极少开会，而且已经变得越来越流于形式。据我所知，它目前唯一的职能就是对共产党在宪法要求其做出决定的事项（尤其是涉及外交政策的事项）上先前做出的决定进行不加讨论的追认。

一切真正的权力都掌握在共产党手中，在约 1.2 亿的总人口中，党员人数约为 60 万人（约 600,000 人，总人口约 120,000,000）。

===Original===

I never came across a Communist by chance: the people whom I met in the streets or in the villages, when I could get into conversation with them, almost invariably said they were of no party. The only other answer I ever had was from some of the peasants, who openly stated that they were Tsarists. It must be said that the peasants’ reasons for disliking the Bolsheviks are very inadequate. It is said﻿—and all I saw confirmed the assertion﻿—that the peasants are better off than they ever were before. I saw no one﻿—man, woman, or child﻿—who looked underfed in the villages. The big landowners are dispossessed, and the peasants have profited. But the towns and the army still need nourishing, and the Government has nothing to give the peasants in return for food except paper, which the peasants resent having to take. It is a singular fact that Tsarist roubles are worth ten times as much as Soviet roubles, and are much commoner in the country. Although they are illegal, pocketbooks full of them are openly displayed in the market places. I do not think it should be inferred that the peasants expect a Tsarist restoration: they are merely actuated by custom and dislike of novelty. They have never heard of the blockade; consequently they cannot understand why the Government is unable to give them the clothes and agricultural implements that they need. Having got their land, and being ignorant of affairs outside their own neighbourhood, they wish their own village to be independent, and would resent the demands of any Government whatever.
Within the Communist Party there are, of course, as always in a bureaucracy, different factions, though hitherto the external pressure has prevented disunion. It seemed to me that the personnel of the bureaucracy could be divided into three classes. There are first the old revolutionists, tested by years of persecution. These men have most of the highest posts. Prison and exile have made them tough and fanatical and rather out of touch with their own country. They are honest men, with a profound belief that Communism will regenerate the world.

===Chinese===

我从未偶然碰见过一名共产党员：在街头或村庄里遇到的那些人，每当我能同他们攀谈起来时，他们几乎毫无例外地表示自己不属于任何党派。我得到的唯一其他回答来自一些农民，他们公开宣称自己是沙皇的支持者。必须指出的是，农民讨厌布尔什维克的理由是非常站不住脚的。据说——而且我所见到的一切都证实了这一说法——农民的生活境况比以往任何时候都要好。在村庄里，我没有看到任何一个人——无论是男人、女人还是儿童——看起来营养不良。大地主被剥夺了土地，农民从中获了利。但城市和军队依然需要粮食维系，而政府除了纸币之外没有任何东西可以提供给农民以换取粮食，农民对不得不接受纸币感到愤慨。一个奇特的事实是，沙皇时期的卢布价值是苏维埃卢布的十倍，并且在农村普遍得多。尽管使用沙皇卢布是非法的，但装满这种货币的皮夹却在集市上公开展示。我认为不应当据此推断农民期待沙皇复辟：他们纯粹是受习惯驱使并厌恶新鲜事物。他们从未听说过封锁（blockade）；因此，他们无法理解为什么政府无法向他们提供所急需的衣物和农具。既然已经分得了土地，又对自身邻里之外的事务一无所知，他们便希望自己的村庄能够保持独立，并会抵制任何政府提出的要求。

在共产党内部，正如在任何官僚体制中一样，自然存在着不同的派系，尽管迄今为止外部压力防止了分裂。在我看来，官僚体制的人员可以划分为三个阶层。首先是经受了多年迫害考验的老革命家。这些人占据着绝大多数最高职位。监狱与流放使他们变得坚强、狂热，并且相当脱离本国的实际国情。他们是诚实的人，深信共产主义（Communism）将使世界重获新生。

===Original===

They think themselves utterly free from sentiment, but, in fact, they are sentimental about Communism and about the regime that they are creating; they cannot face the fact that what they are creating is not complete Communism, and that Communism is anathema to the peasant, who wants his own land and nothing else. They are pitiless in punishing corruption or drunkenness when they find either among officials; but they have built up a system in which the temptations to petty corruption are tremendous, and their own materialistic theory should persuade them that under such a system corruption must be rampant.
The second class in the bureaucracy, among whom are to be found most of the men occupying political posts just below the top, consists of arrivistes, who are enthusiastic Bolsheviks because of the material success of Bolshevism. With them must be reckoned the army of policemen, spies, and secret agents, largely inherited from the Tsarist times, who make their profit out of the fact that no one can live except by breaking the law. This aspect of Bolshevism is exemplified by the Extraordinary Commission, a body practically independent of the Government, possessing its own regiments, who are better fed than the Red Army. This body has the power of imprisoning any man or woman without trial on such charges as speculation or counterrevolutionary activity. It has shot thousands without proper trial, and though now it has nominally lost the power of inflicting the death penalty, it is by no means certain that it has altogether lost it in fact. It has spies everywhere, and ordinary mortals live in terror of it.
The third class in the bureaucracy consists of men who are not ardent Communists, who have rallied to the Government since it has proved itself stable, and who work for it either out of patriotism or because they enjoy the opportunity of developing their ideas freely without the obstacle of traditional institutions. Among this class are to be found men of the type of the successful business man, men with the same sort of ability as is found in the American self-made Trust magnate, but working for success and power, not for money. There is no doubt that the Bolsheviks are successfully solving the problem of enlisting this kind of ability in the public service, without permitting it to amass wealth as it does in capitalist communities.

===Chinese===

他们自认为完全不带感情色彩，但事实上，他们对共产主义以及他们正在创造的政权充满了感伤主义情绪；他们无法正视这样一个事实：他们所创造的并非彻底的共产主义，而且共产主义对那些只想要属于自己的土地而别无所求的农民来说宛如诅咒。当他们在官员中发现贪腐或酗酒行为时，他们会施以毫不留情的惩罚；然而他们建立起来的体制，却滋生着巨大的微小腐败诱惑，他们自己的唯物主义理论本应让他们相信，在这样的体制下腐败必然猖獗泛滥。

官僚体制中的第二个阶层——占据着仅次于最高层政治职位的多数人便属于此类——由投机钻营者（arrivistes）组成，他们之所以成为狂热的布尔什维克，是由于布尔什维主义在物质上取得的成功。与他们归为一类的，还有在很大程度上继承自沙皇时代的大批警察、间谍和特工，他们从“不违法便无法生存”这一事实中牟取私利。布尔什维主义的这一面向由契卡（Cheka，全俄肃反委员会）体现得淋漓尽致，该机构实际上独立于政府，拥有自己的军团，其伙食待遇优于红军（Red Army）。该机构有权以投机倒把或从事反革命（counterrevolutionary）活动等罪名，不经审判便将任何人投入监狱。它未经正当审判就枪决了数以千计的人，尽管如今它名义上已丧失判处死刑的权力，但事实上它是否完全丧失了这一权力则绝不能确定。它的密探遍布各处，普通凡人生活在其恐怖阴影之下。

官僚体制中的第三个阶层由那些并非狂热共产党员的人组成，自政府证明了自身的稳固以来，他们便团结在政府周围，他们为政府效力要么是出于爱国心，要么是因为他们享受能够不受传统制度阻碍、自由施展其抱负的机会。在这一阶层中，可以找到成功商业人士类型的人，他们具备在白手起家的美国托拉斯巨头身上所见的那种能力，但他们为成功和权力劳作，而非为了金钱。毫无疑问，布尔什维克正成功地解决将此类人才吸纳进公共服务中的问题，且不允许他们像在资本主义社会中那样聚敛财富。

===Original===

This is perhaps their greatest success so far, outside the domain of war. It makes it possible to suppose that, if Russia is allowed to have peace, an amazing industrial development may take place, making Russia a rival of the United States. The Bolsheviks are industrialists in all their aims; they love everything in modern industry except the excessive rewards of the capitalists. And the harsh discipline to which they are subjecting the workers is calculated, if anything can, to give them the habits of industry and honesty which have hitherto been lacking, and the lack of which alone prevents Russia from being one of the foremost industrial countries.

===Chinese===

在战争领域之外，这或许是他们迄今为止取得的最大成功。这使得人们有理由设想，如果俄国得以享有和平，可能会出现惊人的工业发展，使俄国成为美国的竞争对手。布尔什维克在他们所有的目标中都是工业主义者；除了资本家过分的回报之外，他们热爱现代工业中的一切。他们正对工人们施加的严酷纪律，如果说还有什么能起作用的话，正是旨在赋予他们迄今为止所欠缺的勤勉与诚信习惯——而唯独这种欠缺阻碍了俄国成为首屈一指的工业国之一。

## VI The Failure of Russian Industry / VI 俄国工业的崩溃
===Original===

At first sight it is surprising that Russian industry should have collapsed as badly as it has done, and still more surprising that the efforts of the Communists have not been more successful in reviving it. As I believe that the continued efficiency of industry is the main condition for success in the transition to a Communist State, I shall endeavour to analyse the causes of the collapse, with a view to the discovery of ways by which it can be avoided elsewhere.
Of the fact of the collapse there can be no doubt. The Ninth Congress of the Communist Party (March﻿–﻿April, 1920) speaks of “the incredible catastrophes of public economy,” and in connection with transport, which is one of the vital elements of the problem, it acknowledges “the terrible collapse of the transport and the railway system,” and urges the introduction of “measures which cannot be delayed and which are to obviate the complete paralysis of the railway system and, together with this, the ruin of the Soviet Republic.” Almost all those who have visited Russia would confirm this view of the gravity of the situation. In the factories, in great works like those of Putilov and Sormovo, very little except war work is being done; machinery stands idle and plant is becoming unusable. One sees hardly any new manufactured articles in Russia, beyond a certain very inadequate quantity of clothes and boots﻿—always excepting what is needed for the army. And the difficulty of obtaining food is conclusive evidence of the absence of goods such as are needed by the peasants.
How has this state of affairs arisen? And why does it continue?
A great deal of disorganization occurred before the first revolution and under Kerensky. Russian industry was partly dependent on Poland; the war was conducted by methods of reckless extravagance, especially as regards rolling-stock; under Kerensky there was a tendency to universal holiday, under the impression that freedom had removed the necessity for work. But when all this is admitted to the full, it remains true that the state of industry under the Bolsheviks is much worse than even under Kerensky.
The first and most obvious reason for this is that Russia was quite unusually dependent upon foreign assistance.

===Chinese===

乍看之下，俄国工业崩溃到如此严重的程度是令人吃惊的，而更令人吃惊的是共产党人复兴工业的努力未能取得更大的成功。由于我相信工业的持续高效是从向共产主义（Communism）国家过渡取得成功的主要条件，我将竭力分析其崩溃的原因，以期发现其他地方可以避免这种情况的途径。

崩溃的事实是不容置疑的。共产党第九次代表大会（1920 年 3—4 月）谈到了“国民经济令人难以置信的灾难”；而在谈到作为该问题关键要素之一的运输时，大会承认了“运输和铁路系统的可怕崩溃”，并敦促采取“刻不容缓的措施，以避免铁路系统的彻底瘫痪，以及随之而来的苏维埃共和国的毁灭”。几乎所有访问过俄国的人都会证实这一局势的严重性。在工厂里，在像普梯洛夫和索尔莫沃这样的大型工厂中，除了军工生产外几乎什么都没做；机器闲置，厂房设备变得无法使用。在俄国，除了极不充足的一定数量的衣服和靴子之外——军需供应始终除外——人们几乎看不到任何新制成的工业品。而获取粮食的困难，则是农民所需商品匮乏的决定性证据。

这种局面是如何产生的？又为何会持续下去？

在第一次革命之前以及在克伦斯基统治时期，就出现了大量的混乱失序。俄国工业在一定程度上依赖于波兰；战争是以极其肆无忌惮的挥霍方式进行的，特别是在机车车辆方面；在克伦斯基统治下，人们普遍产生了一种放假的倾向，误以为自由已经消除了劳动的必要性。但是，即便完全承认这一切，事实依然是：布尔什维克统治下的工业状况甚至比克伦斯基统治下要糟糕得多。

造成这一现象的首要且最显而易见的原因是，俄国过去极不寻常地依赖外国的援助。

===Original===

Not only did the machinery in the factories and the locomotives on the railways come from abroad, but the organizing and technical brains in industry were mainly foreign. When the Entente became hostile to Russia, the foreigners in Russian industry either left the country or assisted counterrevolution. Even those who were in fact loyal naturally became suspect, and could not well be employed in responsible posts, any more than Germans could in England during the war. The native Russians who had technical or business skill were little better; they almost all practised sabotage in the first period of the Bolshevik regime. One hears amusing stories of common sailors frantically struggling with complicated accounts, because no competent accountant would work for the Bolsheviks.
But those days passed. When the Government was seen to be stable, a great many of those who had formerly sabotaged it became willing to accept posts under it, and are now in fact so employed, often at quite exceptional salaries. Their importance is thoroughly realized. One resolution at the above-mentioned Congress says (I quote verbally the unedited document which was given to us in Moscow):

===Chinese===

不仅工厂里的机器和铁路上的机车来自国外，工业中的组织与技术人才也主要来自外国。当协约国（Entente）对俄国采取敌对态度时，俄国工业界的外国人要么离开了这个国家，要么协助进行反革命活动。即便是那些事实上效忠的人，也自然受到了怀疑，无法在负责任的岗位上任职，正如战争期间英国无法任用德国人一样。具有技术或商业技能的俄国本土人员也好不到哪里去；在布尔什维克政权的最初时期，他们几乎全都进行过怠工破坏。人们听说过一些滑稽的故事，普通水兵疯狂地与复杂的账目搏斗，因为没有一个合格的会计愿意为布尔什维克工作。

但那些日子过去了。当看到政府稳固之后，许多以前进行怠工破坏的人开始愿意在政府手下任职，如今实际上也受雇于此，且往往享有极高的薪酬。他们的重要性得到了充分的认识。上述代表大会的一项决议指出（我一字不差地引用在莫斯科发给我们的未经删节的文件）：

===Original===

> Being of opinion that without a scientific organization of industry, even the widest application of compulsory labour service, as the great labour heroism of the working class, will not only fail to secure the establishment of a powerful socialist production, but will also fail to assist the country to free itself from the clutches of poverty﻿—the Congress considers it imperative to register all able specialists of the various departments of public economy and widely to utilize them for the purpose of industrial organization.
> The Congress considers the elucidation for the wide masses of the workers of the tremendous character of the economic problems of the country to be one of the chief problems of industrial and general political agitation and propaganda; and of equal importance to this, technical education, and administrative and scientific technical experience. The Congress makes it obligatory on all the members of the party mercilessly to fight that particular obnoxious form, the ignorant conceit which deems the working class capable of solving all problems without the assistance *in the most responsible cases* of specialists of the bourgeois school, the management. Demagogic elements who speculate on this kind of prejudice in the more backward section of our working classes, can have no place in the ranks of the party of Scientific Socialism.

===Chinese===

“大会认为，如果没有科学的工业组织，即使最广泛地实行强迫劳动义务制，哪怕展现出工人阶级伟大的劳动英雄主义，不仅无法确保建立起强大的社会主义生产，而且也无法帮助国家摆脱贫困的泥潭——大会认为当务之急是对国民经济各部门所有胜任的专门人才进行登记，并广泛利用他们来达到组织工业的目的。

大会认为，向广大工人群众阐明国家经济问题的严重性，是工业和一般政治鼓动与宣传的主要课题之一；与此同等重要的，还有技术教育，以及行政与科学技术经验。大会要求全体党员无情地打击那种特别有害的表现，即那种认为工人阶级有能力在*最负责任的场合*不需要资产阶级学派的专门家和管理人员的协助就能解决一切问题的无知自大。在我们工人阶级较为落后的部分中利用此类偏见进行投机的蛊惑民心分子，在科学社会主义政党的队伍中绝无立足之地。”

===Original===

But Russia alone is unable to supply the amount of skill required, and is very deficient in technical instructors, as well as in skilled workmen. One was told, over and over again, that the first step in improvement would be the obtaining of spare parts for locomotives. It seems strange that these could not be manufactured in Russia. To some extent they can be, and we were shown locomotives which had been repaired on Communist Saturdays. But in the main the machinery for making spare parts is lacking and the skill required for its manufacture does not exist. Thus dependence on the outside world persists, and the blockade continues to do its deadly work of spreading hunger, demoralization and despair.
The food question is intimately bound up with the question of industry. There is a vicious circle, for not only does the absence of manufactured goods cause a food shortage in the towns, but the food shortage, in turn, diminishes the strength of the workers and makes them less able to produce goods. I cannot but think that there has been some mismanagement as regards the food question. For example, in Petrograd many workers have allotments and often work in them for eight hours after an eight hours’ day in their regular employment. But the food produced in the allotments is taken for general consumption, not left to each individual producer. This is in accordance with Communist theory, but of course greatly diminishes the incentive to work, and increases the red tape and administrative machinery.
Lack of fuel has been another very grave source of trouble. Before the war coal came mostly from Poland and the Donets Basin. Poland is lost to Russia, and the Donetz Basin was in the hands of Denikin, who so destroyed the mines before retreating that they are still not in working order. The result is a practically complete absence of coal. Oil, which is equally important in Russia, was also lacking until the recent recovery of Baku. All that I saw on the Volga made me believe that real efficiency has been shown in reorganizing the transport of oil, and doubtless this will do something to revive industry. But the oil used to be worked very largely by Englishmen, and English machinery is much needed for refining it. In the meantime, Russia has had to depend upon wood, which involves immense labour.

===Chinese===

但单凭俄国自身无法提供所需数量的技能人才，不仅技术教员极其匮乏，熟练工人也严重不足。人们被一次又一次地告知，改善的第一步将是获取机车备件。这些备件无法在俄国内制造，似乎令人费解。在某种程度上它们是可以制造的，我们看到了在‘共产主义星期六’（Communist Saturdays）修复的机车。但总的来说，制造备件的机械十分匮乏，而且制造这些机械所需的技能并不存在。因此，对外部世界的依赖依然存在，而封锁（blockade）继续发挥着散播饥饿、颓废与绝望的致命作用。

粮食问题与工业问题紧密交织在一起。这是一个恶性循环，因为不仅工业品的匮乏导致城市缺粮，而且粮食短缺反过来又削弱了工人的体能，使他们更没有能力生产商品。我不能不认为在粮食问题上存在着某种管理不善。例如在彼得格勒（Petrograd），许多工人都分有一块菜地，并且常常在八小时常规工作后在菜地上劳作八小时。但在这些菜地上生产出来的粮食却被征集用于公共消费，而不留给每个具体的生产者。这虽然符合共产主义理论，但当然极大地挫伤了劳动的积极性，并增加了官僚繁文缛节与行政机构的负担。

燃料匮乏是另一个非常严重的困扰根源。战前煤炭主要来自波兰和顿涅茨煤田（Donets Basin）。波兰对俄国而言已经丧失，而顿涅茨煤田此前掌握在邓尼金手中，他在撤退前彻底破坏了矿井，以至于至今仍无法恢复运转。结果就是煤炭几乎彻底断绝。在俄国同样重要的石油，直到最近收复巴库后才得以缓解。我在伏尔加河沿岸所见的一切，使我相信在重组石油运输方面展现出了真正的效率，这无疑将对复兴工业起到一定作用。但过去石油开采在很大程度上由英国人经手，精炼石油急需英国的机械。在此期间，俄国不得不依赖木柴，这耗费了大量的劳动力。

===Original===

Most of the houses are not warmed in winter, so that people live in a temperature below freezing-point. Another consequence of lack of fuel was the bursting of water-pipes, so that people in Petrograd, for the most part, have to go down to the Neva to fetch their water﻿—a considerable addition to the labour of an already overworked day.
I find it difficult to believe that, if greater efficiency had existed in the Government, the food and fuel difficulties could not have been considerably alleviated. In spite of the needs of the army, there are still many horses in Russia; I saw troops of thousands of horses on the Volga, which apparently belonged to Kalmyk tribes. By the help of carts and sledges, it ought to be possible, without more labour than is warranted by the importance of the problem, to bring food and timber into Moscow and Petrograd. It must be remembered that both cities are surrounded by forests, and Moscow at least is surrounded by good agricultural land. The Government has devoted all its best energies hitherto to the two tasks of war and propaganda, while industry and the food problem have been left to a lesser degree of energy and intelligence. It is no doubt probable that, if peace is secured, the economic problems will receive more attention than hitherto. But the Russian character seems less adapted to steady work of an unexciting nature than to heroic efforts on great occasions; it has immense passive endurance, but not much active tenacity. Whether, with the menace of foreign invasion removed, enough day-by-day detailed energy would exist for the reorganization of industry, is a doubtful question, as to which only time can decide.
This leads to the conclusion﻿—which I think is adopted by most of the leading men in Russia﻿—that it will be very difficult indeed to save the revolution without outside economic assistance. Outside assistance from capitalist countries is dangerous to the principles of Communism, as well as precarious from the likelihood of fresh causes of quarrel. But the need of help is urgent, and if the policy of promoting revolution elsewhere were to succeed, it would probably render the nations concerned temporarily incapable of supplying Russian needs. It is, therefore, necessary for Russia to accept the risks and uncertainties involved in attempting to make peace with the Entente and to trade with America.

===Chinese===

大多数房屋在冬季没有供暖，因此人们生活在冰点以下的温度中。燃料匮乏的另一个后果是水管爆裂，因此彼得格勒的居民大部分不得不走到涅瓦河（Neva）去挑水——这为本已过度操劳的一天增添了相当繁重的劳役。

我很难相信，如果政府内部具备更高的行政效率，粮食和燃料的困难本可以得到显著的缓解。尽管有军队的需求，俄国目前依然有许多马匹；我在伏尔加河畔看到了数以千计的成群马匹，显然属于卡尔梅克部落（Kalmyk tribes）。借助大车和雪橇，在不超过该问题重要性所允许的劳动力范围内，把粮食和木材运进莫斯科和彼得格勒应当是完全可能的。必须记住的是，这两座城市都被森林环绕，而且至少莫斯科周边环绕着优良的农田。迄今为止，政府将其全部最优秀的精力都投入到了战争和宣传这两项任务中，而工业和粮食问题则被分配给了较少程度的精力与才智。毫无疑问，如果获得和平，经济问题很可能会得到比以往更多的关注。但俄国人的性格似乎不太适应乏味单调的日常平稳劳作，而更适应在重大关头付出英雄般的努力；它拥有巨大的被动耐力，但缺乏主动的持久韧性。在消除外敌入侵的威胁后，是否会有足够的日复一日、细致入微的精力来重组工业，是一个存疑的问题，对此唯有时间能给出答案。

这就引出了一个结论——我认为这也是俄国大多数主要领导人所赞同的结论——即如果不借助外部的经济援助，挽救革命将确实是非常困难的。来自资本主义国家的外部援助不仅对共产主义原则构成危险，而且由于很可能产生新的争端根源而显得极不稳固。但对援助的需求是迫在眉睫的，如果向其他地方推进革命的政策取得成功，它可能会使相关国家暂时无力供应俄国的需求。因此，俄国必须承担尝试与协约国媾和并与美国通商所包含的风险与不确定性。

===Original===

By continuing war, Russia can do infinite damage to us, especially in Asia, but cannot hope, for many years, to achieve any degree of internal prosperity. The situation, therefore, is one in which, even from the narrowest point of view, peace is to the interest of both parties.
It is difficult for an outsider with only superficial knowledge to judge of the efforts which have been made to reorganize industry without outside help. These efforts have chiefly taken the form of industrial conscription. Workers in towns seek to escape to the country, in order to have enough to eat; but this is illegal and severely punished. The same Communist Report from which I have already quoted speaks on this subject as follows:

> *Labour Desertion.*﻿—Owing to the fact that a considerable part of the workers either in search of better food conditions or often for the purposes of speculation, voluntarily leave their places of employment or change from place to place, which inevitably harms production and deteriorates the general position of the working class, the Congress considers one of the most urgent problems of Soviet Government and of the Trade Union organization to be established as the firm, systematic and insistent struggle with labour desertion. The way to fight this is to publish a list of desertion fines, the creation of a labour Detachment of Deserters under fine, and, finally, internment in concentration camps.

It is hoped to extend the system to the peasantry:

> The defeat of the White Armies and the problems of peaceful construction in connection with the incredible catastrophes of public economy demand an extraordinary effort of all the powers of the proletariat and the drafting into the process of public labour of the wide masses of the peasantry.

On the vital subject of transport, in a passage of which I have already quoted a fragment, the Communist Party declares:

===Chinese===

通过继续进行战争，俄国可以对我们造成无限的破坏，尤其是在亚洲，但在许多年内都无法指望实现任何程度的国内繁荣。因此，局势表明，即便从最狭隘的视角来看，和平也符合双方的利益。

对于一个只有肤浅了解的局外人而言，很难去评判在没有外部援助的情况下重组工业所付出的种种努力。这些努力主要采取了工业劳动征用制的形式。城市里的工人试图逃往农村以求填饱肚子；但这是非法的，会受到严厉的惩处。我前面引述过的那份共产党报告在谈到这个问题时如下写道：

> *擅离劳动岗位。*——鉴于相当一部分工人无论是在寻找更好的伙食条件，还是往往出于投机目的，自愿离开其工作岗位或在不同地点之间迁徙，这不可避免地损害了生产并恶化了工人阶级的总体境况，大会认为苏维埃政府和工会组织最紧迫的任务之一，是确立同擅离劳动岗位行为进行坚决、系统和坚持不懈的斗争。与此作斗争的途径是公布开小差罚金名单、建立服罚开小差者劳动特遣队，以及最终将其关押进集中营。

人们希望将这种制度扩大到农民中间：

> 白军的被击溃以及与国民经济不可思议的灾难相关的和平建设问题，要求集中无产阶级的全部力量，并将广大农民群众吸收进公共劳动进程中。

在至关重要的运输问题上，在一段我先前已经引用过的段落中，共产党宣告：

===Original===

> For the most immediate future transport remains the centre of the attention and the efforts of the Soviet Government. The improvement of transport is the indispensable basis upon which even the most moderate success in all other spheres of production and first of all in the provision question can be gained.
> The chief difficulty with regard to the improvement of transport is the weakness of the Transport Trade Union, which is due in the first case to the heterogeneity of the personnel of the railways, amongst whom there are still a number of those who belong to the period of disorganization, and, secondly, to the fact that the most class-conscious and best elements of the railway proletariat were at the various fronts of the civil war.
> Considering wide Trade Union assistance to the railway workers to be one of the principal tasks of the Party, and as the only condition under which transport can be raised to its height, the Congress at the same time recognizes the inflexible necessity of employing exclusive and extraordinary measures (martial law, and so forth). Such necessity is the result of the terrible collapse of the transport and the railroad system and is to introduce measures which cannot be delayed and which are to obviate the complete paralysis of the railway system and, together with this, the ruin of the Soviet Republic.

The general attitude to the militarization of labour is stated in the Resolution with which this section of the Proceedings begins:

===Chinese===

> 在最近的未来，运输仍然是苏维埃政府关注和努力的中心。运输的改善是不可或缺的基础，只有在这个基础上，在生产的所有其他领域、首先是在粮食供应问题上，才能取得哪怕是最适度的成功。
> 改善运输的主要困难在于运输工会（Transport Trade Union）的软弱，这首先是由于铁路人员构成的杂乱，其中仍有许多人属于失序时期的分子；其次是由于铁路无产阶级中最有阶级意识的（class-conscious）和最优秀的分子都在国内战争的各个前线。
> 大会认为工会对铁路工人广泛的协助是本党的主要任务之一，并且是提高运输水平的唯一条件，同时大会承认采取排他性和非常措施（戒严等等）的不可动摇的必要性。这种必要性是运输和铁路系统可怕崩溃的结果，必须采取刻不容缓的措施，以避免铁路系统的彻底瘫痪，以及随之而来的苏维埃共和国的毁灭。

对待劳动军事化的一般态度，表述在该节议事日程开头的决议中：

===Original===

> The ninth Congress approves of the decision of the Central Committee of the Russian Communist Party on the mobilization of the industrial proletariat, compulsory labour service, militarization of production and the application of military detachments to economic needs.
> In connection with the above, the Congress decrees that the Party organization should in every way assist the Trade Unions and the Labour Sections in registering all skilled workers with a view of employing them in the various branches of production with the same consistency and strictness as was done, and is being carried out at the present time, in relation to the commanding staff for army needs.
> Every skilled worker is to return to his particular trade. Exceptions, i.e. the retention of the skilled worker in any other branch of Soviet service, is allowed only with the sanction of the corresponding central and local authorities.

It is, of course, evident that in these measures the Bolsheviks have been compelled to travel a long way from the ideals which originally inspired the revolution. But the situation is so desperate that they could not be blamed if their measures were successful. In a shipwreck all hands must turn to, and it would be ridiculous to prate of individual liberty. The most distressing feature of the situation is that these stern laws seem to have produced so little effect. Perhaps in the course of years Russia might become self-supporting without help from the outside world, but the suffering meantime would be terrible. The early hopes of the revolution would fade more and more. Every failure of industry, every tyrannous regulation brought about by the desperate situation, is used by the Entente as a justification of its policy. If a man is deprived of food and drink, he will grow weak, lose his reason, and finally die. This is not usually considered a good reason for inflicting death by starvation. But where nations are concerned, the weakness and struggles are regarded as morally culpable, and are held to justify further punishment. So at least it has been in the case of Russia. Nothing produced a doubt in our governing minds as to the rightness of our policy except the strength of the Red Army and the fear of revolution in Asia. Is it surprising that professions of humanitarian feeling on the part of English people are somewhat coldly received in Soviet Russia?

===Chinese===

> 第九次代表大会赞同俄国共产党中央委员会关于动员工业无产阶级、实行强迫劳动义务制、生产军事化以及将军队编队用于经济需求的决定。
> 结合上述情况，大会决定，党组织应当在各个方面协助工会和劳动部门对所有熟练工人进行登记，以便在各个生产部门中以如同对待、且目前正在对待军队指挥人员需求一样的连贯性与严格性来使用他们。
> 每一位熟练工人都必须回到其专门行当。唯有获得相应中央和地方当局的批准，才允许有例外，即使该熟练工人留任于苏维埃服务的任何其他部门。

显而易见，在这些措施中，布尔什维克被迫远离了最初激励革命的理想。但局势如此绝望，如果他们的措施能够成功，他们是无可指责的。在发生海难时，所有人必须全力以赴抢险，空谈个人自由将是荒唐可笑的。局势中最令人痛苦的特征是，这些严酷的法律似乎收效甚微。也许在未来的岁月里，俄国在没有外部世界帮助的情况下可以变得自给自足，但在此期间所承受的痛苦将是可怕的。革命早期的希望将越来越黯淡。工业的每一次失败、由绝境所致的每一条专制法令，都被协约国用作其政策合理性的借口。如果一个人被剥夺了食物和饮水，他就会衰弱、丧失理智并最终死亡。通常这并不被认为是施加饥饿致死惩罚的正当理由。但一涉及国家，这种衰弱和挣扎就被视为道德上有罪，并被认为证明了进一步惩罚的合理性。至少在俄国的问题上就是如此。除了红军（Red Army）的力量和对亚洲爆发革命的恐惧之外，没有任何东西能在我们统治者的心中对我们自身政策的正确性产生哪怕一丝怀疑。在苏维埃俄国，英国人自称的人道主义情感遭到冷遇，难道有什么好奇怪的吗？

## VII Daily Life in Moscow / VII 莫斯科的日常生活
===Original===

Daily life in Moscow, so far as I could discover, has neither the horrors depicted by the Northcliffe Press nor the delights imagined by the more ardent of our younger Socialists.
On the one hand, there is no disorder, very little crime, not much insecurity for those who keep clear of politics. Everybody works hard; the educated people have, by this time, mostly found their way into Government offices or teaching or some other administrative profession in which their education is useful. The theatres, the opera and the ballet continue as before, and are quite admirable; some of the seats are paid for, others are given free to members of trade unions. There is, of course, no drunkenness, or at any rate so little that none of us ever saw a sign of it. There is very little prostitution, infinitely less than in any other capital. Women are safer from molestation than anywhere else in the world. The whole impression is one of virtuous, well-ordered activity.
On the other hand, life is very hard for all except men in good posts. It is hard, first of all, owing to the food shortage. This is familiar to all who have interested themselves in Russia, and it is unnecessary to dwell upon it. What is less realized is that most people work much longer hours than in this country. The eight-hour day was introduced with a flourish of trumpets; then, owing to the pressure of the war, it was extended to ten hours in certain trades. But no provision exists against extra work at other jobs, and very many people do extra work, because the official rates do not afford a living wage. This is not the fault of the Government, at any rate as regards the major part; it is due chiefly to war and blockade. When the day’s work is over, a great deal of time has to be spent in fetching food and water and other necessaries of life. The sight of the workers going to and fro, shabbily clad, with the inevitable bundle in one hand and tin can in the other, through streets almost entirely empty of traffic, produces the effect of life in some vast village, rather than in an important capital city.
Holidays, such as are common throughout all but the very poorest class in this country, are very difficult in Russia. A train journey requires a permit, which is only granted on good reasons being shown; with the present shortage of transport, this regulation is quite unavoidable.

===Chinese===

就我所能发现的而言，莫斯科（Moscow）的日常生活既没有诺思克利夫报系所描绘的那种恐怖，也没有我们年轻热诚的社会主义者所想象的那种愉悦。

一方面，这里没有混乱，极少有犯罪，对于不卷入政治的人而言也没有多少不安全感。每个人都在努力工作；受过教育的人到此时大部分已进入政府机构、教学岗位或其他能够发挥其受教育长处的行政行业。剧院、歌剧和芭蕾舞如往常一样继续上演，且相当精彩；部分门票是收费的，其余门票则免费发放给工会（trade unions）会员。当然，这里没有酗酒现象，或者无论如何其程度极其轻微，以至于我们从未见过任何迹象。卖淫现象极少，远少于任何其他国家的首都。妇女免遭骚扰的安全性高于世界上任何其他地方。整体印象是一种道德高尚、井然有序的忙碌活动。

另一方面，对于除了身居要职之人以外的所有人来说，生活是非常艰难的。首先，由于粮食短缺，生活极为艰难。这对所有关心俄国局势的人来说都是耳熟能详的，无需赘言。人们较少认识到的是，大多数人的工作时间比在我国要长得多。八小时工作制曾在高调宣扬下引入；随后，由于战争的压力，在某些行业被延长到了十小时。但对于在其他岗位从事兼职并没有任何限制规定，非常多的人都在从事兼职工作，因为官方规定的薪酬无法维持基本的生活生计。这绝非完全是政府的过错，至少主要部分不是；这主要是由于战争和封锁造成的。当一天的劳作结束时，人们不得不耗费大量时间去打水、取粮以及寻找其他生活必需品。看着工人们衣衫褴褛、手里不可避免地一只手提着包袱、另一只手拎着铁罐，穿过几乎完全没有车辆通行的街道来来往往，这给人带来的感觉仿佛是置身于某个巨大的村庄，而非一座重要的首都大都市。

像在我国除了极少数最贫困阶层之外普遍享有的休假，在俄国是极其困难的。乘坐火车需要通行许可证，只有在出示充分理由的情况下才会获批；鉴于目前的运输短缺，这项规定是完全不可避免的。

===Original===

Railway queues are a common feature in Moscow; it often takes several days to get a permit. Then, when it has been obtained, it may take several more days to get a seat in a train. The ordinary trains are inconceivably crowded, far more so, though that seems impossible, than London trains at the busiest hour. On the shorter journeys, passengers are even known to ride on the roof and buffers, or cling like flies to the sides of the wagons. People in Moscow travel to the country whenever they can afford the time and get a permit, because in the country there is enough to eat. They go to stay with relations﻿—most people in Moscow, in all classes, but especially among manual workers, have relations in the country. One cannot, of course, go to an hotel as one would in other countries. Hotels have been taken over by the State, and the rooms in them (when they are still used) are allocated by the police to people whose business is recognized as important by the authorities. Casual travel is therefore impossible even on a holiday.
Journeys have vexations in addition to the slowness and overcrowding of the trains. Police search the travellers for evidences of “speculation,” especially for food. The police play, altogether, a much greater part in daily life than they do in other countries﻿—much greater than they did, for example, in Prussia twenty-five years ago, when there was a vigorous campaign against Socialism. Everybody breaks the law almost daily, and no one knows which among his acquaintances is a spy of the Extraordinary Commission. Even in the prisons, among prisoners, there are spies, who are allowed certain privileges but not their liberty.
Newspapers are not taken in, except by very few people, but they are stuck up in public places, where passersby occasionally glance at them.5 There is very little to read; owing to paper shortage, books are rare, and money to buy them is still rarer. One does not see people reading, as one does here in the Underground for example. There is practically no social life, partly because of the food shortage, partly because, when anybody is arrested, the police are apt to arrest everybody whom they find in his company, or who comes to visit him. And once arrested, a man or woman, however innocent, may remain for months in prison without trial.

===Chinese===

火车站排长队是莫斯科的常见景象；获取一张通行证往往需要花费数天时间。随后，即便拿到了通行证，可能还需要再等几天才能在火车上获得一个座位。普通列车的拥挤程度令人难以想象，即便看似不可能，却远甚于伦敦高峰时段的列车。在较短的旅程中，甚至有人骑在车顶和缓冲器上，或者像苍蝇一样紧贴在车厢两侧。莫斯科人只要能抽得出时间并拿到许可证，就会前往乡村，因为在农村有足够的食物。他们去投奔亲戚——莫斯科各阶层的大多数人，尤其是体力劳动者，在农村都有亲戚。人们当然不能像在其他国家那样住进旅馆。旅馆已被国家接管，其中的房间（在仍在使用时）由警方分配给那些公务被当局认定为重要的人员。因此，即便在假期，随性的旅行也是不可能的。

除了列车的缓慢与过度拥挤之外，旅途还有其他令人恼火之处。警察会搜查旅客以寻找“投机倒把”的证据，特别是搜查食物。总的来说，警察在日常生活中扮演的角色比在其他国家大得多——例如，远大于二十五年前普鲁士严厉打击社会主义时期警察所扮演的角色。每个人几乎每天都在违法，而且没有人知道自己的熟人中谁是契卡（Cheka，全俄肃反委员会）的密探。即便在监狱的犯人当中，也有密探存在，他们享有某些特权，只是没有获得人身自由。

除了极少数人外，普通人并不订购报纸，但报纸会被张贴在公共场所，路过的行人偶尔会看上一眼。5 几乎没有什么读物；由于纸张短缺，书籍非常罕见，而购买书籍的钱财则更为稀缺。人们看不到像在伦敦地铁里那样捧书阅读的情景。实际上几乎没有任何社交生活，部分是由于食物短缺，部分是因为每当有人被捕时，警察往往会逮捕在其身边发现的每一个人，或者前来探望他的每一个人。而一旦被捕，一个人无论多么无辜，都可能在未经审判的情况下在监狱中被关押数月之久。

===Original===

While we were in Moscow, forty social revolutionaries and Anarchists were hunger-striking to enforce their demand to be tried and to be allowed visits. I was told that on the eighth day of the strike the Government consented to try them, and that few could be proved guilty of any crime; but I had no means of verifying this.
Industrial conscription is, of course, rigidly enforced. Every man and woman has to work, and slacking is severely punished, by prison or a penal settlement. Strikes are illegal, though they sometimes occur. By proclaiming itself the friend of the proletarian, the Government has been enabled to establish an iron discipline, beyond the wildest dreams of the most autocratic American magnate. And by the same professions the Government has led Socialists from other countries to abstain from reporting unpleasant features in what they have seen.
The Tolstoyans, of whom I saw the leaders, are obliged by their creed to resist every form of conscription, though some have found ways of compromising. The law concerning conscientious objectors to military service is practically the same as ours, and its working depends upon the temper of the tribunal before which a man comes. Some conscientious objectors have been shot; on the other hand, some have obtained absolute exemption.
Life in Moscow, as compared to life in London, is drab, monotonous, and depressed. I am not, of course, comparing life there with that of the rich here, but with that of the average working-class family. When it is realized that the highest wages are about fifteen shillings a month, this is not surprising. I do not think that life could, under any system, be very cheerful in a country so exhausted by war as Russia, so I am not saying this as a criticism of the Bolsheviks. But I do think there might be less police interference, less vexatious regulation, and more freedom for spontaneous impulses towards harmless enjoyments.
Religion is still very strong. I went into many churches, where I saw obviously famished priests in gorgeous vestments, and a congregation enormously devout. Generally more than half the congregation were men, and among the men many were soldiers. This applies to the towns as well as to the country. In Moscow I constantly saw people in the streets crossing themselves.

===Chinese===

我们在莫斯科期间，有四十名社会革命党人和无政府主义者正在绝食，以迫使当局满足他们接受审判和获准探视的要求。有人告诉我，在绝食的第八天，政府同意对他们进行审判，并且几乎没有人能被证实有罪；但我无法核实这一点。

工业劳动征用制自然得到了严格执行。每个男人和女人都必须工作，怠工会受到严厉惩罚，会被关入监狱或劳役营。罢工是非法的，尽管有时仍会发生。通过宣称自己是无产阶级的挚友，政府得以建立起一套铁的纪律，这超越了哪怕最专制的美国巨头最狂野的梦想。而正是通过同样的表白，政府引导来自其他国家的社会主义者对其所见的不快之处讳莫如深。

我见到了托尔斯泰主义者（Tolstoyans）的领导人，他们的信条迫使他们抵制任何形式的征兵，尽管有些人已经找到了妥协的方法。关于基于良心拒绝服兵役者的法律与我们的法律大致相同，其实施取决于当事人所面对的法庭的性情。一些基于良心拒服兵役者遭到了枪决；另一方面，有些人则获得了完全的免除。

与伦敦的生活相比，莫斯科的生活是单调、乏味且压抑的。我当然不是拿那里的生活与这里富人的生活相比，而是与普通工人阶级家庭相比。当人们意识到最高工资大约只有每月 15 先令时，这就不足为奇了。我认为在俄国这样一个因战争而极度疲惫的国家，无论在何种制度下生活都不可能非常愉悦，因此我这么说并非出于对布尔什维克的批评。但我确实认为，警察的干预可以少一些，令人恼火的规管可以少一些，而对无害享乐的自发冲动应当给予更多的自由。

宗教依然非常强大。我走进许多教堂，在那里我看到衣着华丽但明显处于饥饿之中的神父，以及极其虔诚的会众。会众中通常有一半以上是男性，在男性中有许多是军人。这既适用于城市，也适用于乡村。在莫斯科的街道上，我经常看到人们在胸前画十字。

===Original===

There is a theory that the Moscow working man feels himself free from capitalist domination, and therefore bears hardships gladly. This is no doubt true of the minority who are active Communists, but I do not think it has any truth for the others. The average working man, to judge by a rather hasty impression, feels himself the slave of the Government, and has no sense whatever of having been liberated from a tyranny.
I recognize to the full the reasons for the bad state of affairs, in the past history of Russia and the recent policy of the Entente. But I have thought it better to record impressions frankly, trusting the readers to remember that the Bolsheviks have only a very limited share of responsibility for the evils from which Russia is suffering.

===Chinese===

有一种理论认为，莫斯科的工人阶级觉得自己摆脱了资本主义的统治，因此心甘情愿地承受艰难困苦。对于作为积极共产党员的少数人而言，这无疑是事实，但我认为对于其他人而言则并非如此。根据一种颇为仓促的印象来判断，普通的劳动者觉得自己是政府的奴隶，丝毫没有从暴政中获得解放的感觉。

我完全承认造成这种恶劣局面的原因，这深植于俄国的过去历史以及协约国（Entente）近期的政策之中。但我认为坦率地记录这些印象更好，我相信读者会记住，对于俄国正在承受的种种苦难，布尔什维克只负有非常有限的一部分责任。

## VIII Town and Country / VIII 城市与乡村
===Original===

The problem of inducing the peasants to feed the towns is one which Russia shares with Central Europe, and from what one hears Russia has been less unsuccessful than some other countries in dealing with this problem. For the Soviet Government, the problem is mainly concentrated in Moscow and Petrograd; the other towns are not very large, and are mostly in the centre of rich agricultural districts. It is true that in the North even the rural population normally depends upon food from more southerly districts; but the northern population is small. It is commonly said that the problem of feeding Moscow and Petrograd is a transport problem, but I think this is only partially true. There is, of course, a grave deficiency of rolling-stock, especially of locomotives in good repair. But Moscow is surrounded by very good land. In the course of a day’s motoring in the neighbourhood, I saw enough cows to supply milk to the whole child population of Moscow, although what I had come to see was children’s sanatoria, not farms. All kinds of food can be bought in the market at high prices. I travelled over a considerable extent of Russian railways, and saw a fair number of goods trains. For all these reasons, I feel convinced that the share of the transport problem in the food difficulties has been exaggerated. Of course transport plays a larger part in the shortage in Petrograd than in Moscow, because food comes mainly from south of Moscow. In Petrograd, most of the people one sees in the streets show obvious signs of under-feeding. In Moscow, the visible signs are much less frequent, but there is no doubt that under-feeding, though not actual starvation, is nearly universal.
The Government supplies rations to everyone who works in the towns at a very low fixed price. The official theory is that the Government has a monopoly of the food and that the rations are sufficient to sustain life. The fact is that the rations are not sufficient, and that they are only a portion of the food supply of Moscow. Moreover, people complain, I do not know how truly, that the rations are delivered irregularly; some say, about every other day. Under these circumstances, almost everybody, rich or poor, buys food in the market, where it costs about fifty times the fixed Government price. A pound of butter costs about a month’s wages.

===Chinese===

促使农民向城市提供粮食的问题，是俄国与中欧共同面临的问题，而据人们所闻，俄国在处理这一问题上比其他某些国家还要少一些失败。对于苏维埃（Soviet）政府而言，这一问题主要集中在莫斯科（Moscow）和彼得格勒（Petrograd）；其他城镇规模都不是很大，且大多位于富饶农业区的中心。诚然，在北部，即便农村人口通常也依赖来自偏南地区的粮食；但北部人口并不多。人们通常说莫斯科和彼得格勒的粮食供应问题是一个运输问题，但我认为这只是部分属实。当然，铁路车辆严重短缺，特别是状况良好的机车更是匮乏。但莫斯科周边环绕着极其优良的土地。在邻近地区乘车游览的一天中，我看到的奶牛足够向莫斯科的全体儿童供应牛奶，尽管我当时去参观的是儿童疗养所而非农场。在集市上可以以高价买到各种食物。我在俄国铁路网上走过了相当长的路程，看到了相当数量的货运列车。鉴于所有这些原因，我深信运输问题在粮食困难中所占的比重被夸大了。当然，与莫斯科相比，运输在彼得格勒的匮乏中扮演了更大的角色，因为粮食主要来自莫斯科以南。在彼得格勒，街上看到的多数人都有明显的营养不良迹象。在莫斯科，这种肉眼可见的迹象要少得多，但毫无疑问的是，营养不良——尽管并非实际上的彻底挨饿——几乎是普遍存在的。

政府以极低的统购统销价格向城市里所有工作的人供应配给粮。官方的理论是政府垄断了粮食供应，而且配给足以维持生命。实际情况是配给并不充足，而且只占莫斯科粮食供应的一部分。此外，人们抱怨配给的发放极不规律，我不知道这有多属实，有人说大约隔天发放一次。在这些情况下，几乎每个人，无论贫富，都在市场上购买食物，而市场上的价格大约是政府固定价格的五十倍。一磅黄油的价格大约相当于一个月的工资。

===Original===

In order to be able to afford extra food, people adopt various expedients. Some do additional work, at extra rates, after their official day’s work is over. For, though there is supposed to be by law an eight-hours day, extended to ten in certain vital industries, the wage paid for it is not a living wage, and there is nothing to prevent a man from undertaking other work in his spare time. But the usual resource is what is called “speculation,” i.e., buying and selling. Some person formerly rich sells clothes or furniture or jewellery in return for food; the buyer sells again at an enhanced price, and so on through perhaps twenty hands, until a final purchaser is found in some well-to-do peasant or nouveau riche speculator. Again, most people have relations in the country, whom they visit from time to time, bringing back with them great bags of flour. It is illegal for private persons to bring food into Moscow, and the trains are searched; but, by corruption or cunning, experienced people can elude the search. The food market is illegal, and is raided occasionally; but as a rule it is winked at. Thus the attempt to suppress private commerce has resulted in an amount of unprofessional buying and selling which far exceeds what happens in capitalist countries. It takes up a great deal of time that might be more profitably employed; and, being illegal, it places practically the whole population of Moscow at the mercy of the police. Moreover, it depends largely upon the stores of goods belonging to those who were formerly rich, and when these are expended the whole system must collapse, unless industry has meanwhile been reestablished on a sound basis.
It is clear that the state of affairs is unsatisfactory, but, from the Government’s point of view, it is not easy to see what ought to be done. The urban and industrial population is mainly concerned in carrying on the work of government and supplying munitions to the army. These are very necessary tasks, the cost of which ought to be defrayed out of taxation. A moderate tax in kind on the peasants would easily feed Moscow and Petrograd. But the peasants take no interest in war or government. Russia is so vast that invasion of one part does not touch another part; and the peasants are too ignorant to have any national consciousness, such as one takes for granted in England or France or Germany.

===Chinese===

为了能够负担额外的食物，人们采取了各种应急手段。有些人会在官方规定的全天工作结束后，从事报酬更高的额外工作。因为尽管法律上规定了八小时工作制（在某些关键行业延长至十小时），但为此支付的工资并不能维持生计，而且没有任何规定阻止一个人在闲暇时间从事其他工作。但最常见的门路是所谓的“投机倒把”，即倒买倒卖。某个从前的富人出售衣物、家具或珠宝以换取食物；买家又以更高的价格转手卖出，如此可能经手二十人之多，直到最终在某个富裕农民或暴发户投机商手中找到买主。此外，大多数人在农村都有亲戚，他们不时前往探望，带回大袋大袋的面粉。私人将粮食带入莫斯科是非法的，火车会遭到搜查；但通过行贿或狡黠，经验丰富的人往往能逃避搜查。食品市场是非法的，偶尔也会遭到查抄；但通常情况下官方对此是睁一只眼闭一只眼的。因此，压制私人商业的尝试反而导致了大量远超资本主义国家发生的非职业买卖。这耗费了大量本可用于更有成效用途的时间；而且由于是非法的，它实际上使整个莫斯科人口都受制于警察的支配。此外，这在很大程度上依赖于旧富人所拥有的物资存量，一旦这些存量耗尽，整个体制便必定崩溃，除非工业在此期间已在健全的基础上重建起来。

显而易见，这种局面是不令人满意的，但从政府的角度来看，很难看清究竟应当做些什么。城市与工业人口主要从事维系政府运转以及为军队提供弹药的工作。这些都是非常必要的任务，其成本理应由税收来承担。向农民征收适度的实物税，本可以轻而易举地养活莫斯科和彼得格勒。但农民对战争或政府毫无兴趣。俄国是如此广袤，以至于入侵某一地区并不会触及另一地区；而且农民过于无知，以至于不具备任何国家意识——而这在英国、法国或德国是被视为理所当然的。

===Original===

The peasants will not willingly part with a portion of their produce merely for purposes of national defence, but only for the goods they need﻿—clothes, agricultural implements, etc.﻿—which the Government, owing to the war and the blockade, is not in a position to supply.
When the food shortage was at its worst, the Government antagonized the peasants by forced requisitions, carried out with great harshness by the Red Army. This method has been modified, but the peasants still part unwillingly with their food, as is natural in view of the uselessness of paper and the enormously higher prices offered by private buyers.
The food problem is the main cause of popular opposition to the Bolsheviks, yet I cannot see how any popular policy could have been adopted. The Bolsheviks are disliked by the peasants because they take so much food; they are disliked in the towns because they take so little. What the peasants want is what is called free trade, i.e., decontrol of agricultural produce. If this policy were adopted, the towns would be faced by utter starvation, not merely by hunger and hardship. It is an entire misconception to suppose that the peasants cherish any hostility to the Entente. The *Daily News* of July 13th, in an otherwise excellent leading article, speaks of “the growing hatred of the Russian peasant, who is neither a Communist nor a Bolshevik, for the Allies generally and this country in particular.” The typical Russian peasant has never heard of the Allies or of this country; he does not know that there is a blockade; all he knows is that he used to have six cows but the Government reduced him to one for the sake of poorer peasants, and that it takes his corn (except what is needed for his own family) at a very low price. The reasons for these actions do not interest him, since his horizon is bounded by his own village. To a remarkable extent, each village is an independent unit. So long as the Government obtains the food and soldiers that it requires, it does not interfere, and leaves untouched the old village communism, which is extraordinarily unlike Bolshevism and entirely dependent upon a very primitive stage of culture.

===Chinese===

农民不会心甘情愿地仅仅为了国防目的就交出一部分农产品，他们只愿意用粮食换取自己所需的商品——衣服、农具等等——而政府由于战争和封锁（blockade），根本无力提供这些物资。

在粮食短缺最为严重的时期，政府通过红军（Red Army）以极其严酷的手段执行余粮强征，从而激化了与农民的矛盾。这种方法后来有所缓和，但农民依然不情愿交出粮食，鉴于纸币的一文不值以及私人买家开出的高得多的价格，这是很自然的。
